It has been a very persistent struggle for me."

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ anxiety_symptoms\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Chronic Worry: Worry occurs more days than not for at least 6 months.\n  - Generalized Worry: Worry is not confined to specific topics but spreads to various domains like work, school, family, and finances.\n  - Catastrophizing: Jumping to conclusions that minor mistakes or events will lead to irreversible disasters.\n  - Intolerance of Uncertainty: Unable to tolerate future uncertainty, trapped in 'what if' questions.\n  - Failure to Control: Unable to regulate worry despite efforts to stop or distract oneself.\n  - Intrusive Worry: Worries suddenly intrude into the mind and interfere with focus while doing other tasks.\n  - Chaining of Worry: One worry triggers another in a chain reaction, escalating to catastrophic thoughts.\n  - Feeling Keyed Up: Feeling anxious and wound up like a spring, unable to stay still.\n  - Physical Tension: The entire body feels stiff and tense, unable to relax.\n  - Nervousness: Nerves are on edge, being startled easily by minor sounds or stimuli.\n  - Low Frustration Tolerance: Getting annoyed immediately at trivial stimuli that would normally be ignored.\n  - Snappiness: Nerves are sharp, leading to sensitive and aggressive reactions to small matters.\n  - Exhausted Patience: Patience is depleted, making it impossible to endure minor inconveniences or delays.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 9 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Chronic Worry: Worry occurs more days than not for at least 6 months.\n  - Generalized Worry: Worry is not confined to specific topics but spreads to various domains like work, school, family, and finances.\n  - Catastrophizing: Jumping to conclusions that minor mistakes or events will lead to irreversible disasters.\n  - Intolerance of Uncertainty: Unable to tolerate future uncertainty, trapped in 'what if' questions.\n  - Failure to Control: Unable to regulate worry despite efforts to stop or distract oneself.\n  - Intrusive Worry: Worries suddenly intrude into the mind and interfere with focus while doing other tasks.\n  - Chaining of Worry: One worry triggers another in a chain reaction, escalating to catastrophic thoughts.\n  - Feeling Keyed Up: Feeling anxious and wound up like a spring, unable to stay still.\n  - Physical Tension: The entire body feels stiff and tense, unable to relax.\n  - Nervousness: Nerves are on edge, being startled easily by minor sounds or stimuli.\n  - Low Frustration Tolerance: Getting annoyed immediately at trivial stimuli that would normally be ignored.\n  - Snappiness: Nerves are sharp, leading to sensitive and aggressive reactions to small matters.\n  -
